```
def score_array(a):
    n = a.size
    if n==0 or sum<=0: return inf
    c = ffi_convolve(a)
    return 2.0*n*c.max() / (a.sum()**2)
```
But for speed in iterations, normalize sum=1 and compute max(c)*2*n. We can skip denominator. Use `score_normalized(a)` where a sum1.

FFT convolution:
```
def fft_convolve(a):
    n=a.size
    L=1
    target=2*n-1
    while L<target: L*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:target]
    # remove tiny negatives
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    return c
```
For n up to 8192, L=16384, fft ~millisec. Many iterations okay.

Need better internal score: For optimization, use logsumexp maybe but max exact via c. c from FFT can have small negative; taking max of positive. Floating errors may make max slightly above true; okay for local. For final, call exact evaluate_sequence.

```
def multiplicative_update(a, steps, seed):
    # a normalized sum1
    best_sc = score_norm(a)
    for it in range(...):
       c=ffi_convolve(a)
       M=c.max()
       if not finite break
       # weights: top k? Maybe use exponential of c/M -1
       lam = adaptive e.g. 20
       w = np.exp(lam*(c/M -1.0))
       # optionally zero small weights
       grad = grad_active(a,w)  # length n
       scale=grad.max()+1e-300
       # choose eta small: maybe 0.01-0.2
       a_new = a * np.exp(-eta*grad/scale)
       # ensure positive; if many zeros, floor? Use np.maximum(a_new,1e-300)
       a_mod = a_new / a_new.sum()
       sc=score_norm(a_mod)
       if sc<best_sc: ...
       else attenuate eta
```

```
while time < deadline and iters < max:
    f_peak = score_coeff(f) # max c (sum1)
    M = f_peak
    f_scaled = f / sqrt(M)  # peak1
    g = solve_lp(f_scaled)
    if g is None: break or use heuristic
    if sum(g) <= 0: break
    g_norm = g/sum(g)
    # line search t
    best_t=0; best_sc=current
    for t in [0.05,0.1,...,1]:
       cand=(1-t)*f+t*g_norm
       sc=score_norm(cand)
       if sc<best_sc: ...
    if best_t>0: f=cand_best
       if exact evaluate improve: update best
    else break
```

Define `grad_soft(a, p)`:
```
L=next power of two >= 2n-1
af = rfft(a,L)
pr = p[::-1]
pf = rfft(pr,L)
conv = irfft(af*pf, L)[:2*n-1]
w = 2.0 * conv[2*n-2::-1]  # length n
```
Check for p uniform: conv_i = Σ_j a_j p_{2n-2 -? wait earlier grad[i]=2 conv[2n-2-i]. Good.

Algorithm:
```
a normalized sum1
repeat:
 c=conv(a,a); M=max
 p = exp(λ(c/M-1))
 q = cross_corr(a,p)  # Σ a_j p_{i+j}
 # to equalize q, update a_new = a * (q.mean()/q)^alpha
 # clip, normalize
```
This is multiplicative gradient with q proportional to gradient (no 2). It may converge to KKT for current p. If p fixed, fixed point q constant; then weighted sum minimized (at least local). If p adapts to top coefficients, hopefully max decreases.

Pseudo:
```
def flatten_iteration(a, lam, alpha):
    c=fft_conv(a); M=max(c)
    if M<=0 return
    p = np.exp(lam*(c/M - 1.0))
    # normalize p? Not necessary but affects q scale. Use p normalized sum=1 for stable.
    p /= p.sum()
    q = cross_corr(a,p)*2? (constant factor irrelevant)
    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
    a_new = a * (q.mean()/q)**alpha
    a_new = normalize(a_new)
    return a_new
```
If α=1, this solves q constant exactly? It sets a_i proportional 1/q_i, but q itself depends on a, so not one-step. Can iterate with same p several times until q stable, then update p. This is like Sinkhorn. For fixed p, alternating multiplicative updates minimize Q_p? The log update a_i *= (mean/q_i)^α is coordinate scaling converging if B_p positive? Might.

Adaptive p: Use `lam` to emphasize top. If lam large, p active; if lam small, p weights all coefficients. Maybe start with moderate λ=20, iterate.

This method may be faster than gradient and no step line search. Need evaluate score each update; accept if improves. If not, reduce α or λ.

Let's combine:
- `flatten_search(a, budget)`:
   lam=20, alpha=0.5
   best_sc=score(a)
   for iters:
      for sub in range(5): cand=apply_iteration(a,lam,alpha); sc=score(cand); if sc<best update a=cand, best; else α*=0.7, maybe lower lam
      if no improvement, change lam/alpha, random perturbation.
- Periodically exact evaluate.

This method resembles "multiplicative weights algorithm for tensor rank" and might find a good optimum.

Let's design hyperparameter schedule:
- λ (sharpness) controls p. We can run several λ values: 5, 10, 20, 50, 100. For each, iterate until stagnation; keep best.
- α (step) between 0.3 and 1.0. If α=1, update aggressive. Use annealing: start α=0.8, decrease on stagnation.
- After a stagnation (e.g., 50 iterations no score improvement <1e-6), perturb: multiply random block by exp(0.1), or add noise to log; then continue.

This local search may be effective. Let's consider computational cost: Each iteration: FFT conv (rfft+mul+irfft) ~L logL. Cross corr another FFT of p (depends on lam) plus mul+irfft. We can optimize: p changes each iteration, need rfft. Two FFTs. Still okay. Could reuse c? no.

We can implement a fixed-point inner loop for a given p: apply update until q relative variation small, then recompute p. But if p changes too often, may not converge. We can alternate.

Pseudo-code:
```
def optimize_flatten(a, time_limit, rng, lam, alpha):
    a=normalize(a)
    best=score(a)
    no_improve=0
    while time...:
       c=conv(a); M=c.max(); p=softmax_top(c,M,lam); p/=p.sum()
       # several inner iterations with same p
       for it in range(10):
           q=cross_corr(a,p)
           qmax=np.max(q); qmin=np.min(q)
           if qmin>0 and (qmax/qmin -1) < 1e-4: break
           a_new = a * (q.mean()/q)**alpha
           a_new = normalize(a_new, clip)
           sc=score(a_new)
           if sc<best*h(1e-8? no relative) and sc<score(a):
                a=a_new; best=sc; no_improve=0
           else:
                # try smaller alpha? or update anyway if does not worsen? To escape?
                alpha *= 0.8
                break
       # after p changes, if no improve, perturb
```

- Endpoint ratio: scale first or last m by factor.

Let's flesh out flatten search function.

```
def flatten_local_search(a, lam, alpha, iters_limit, time_limit, rng, print_tag=''):
    a = orient(normalize(a))
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = score_fast(a)
    cur_a = a.copy()
    bad = 0
    alpha_cur = alpha
    lam_cur = lam
    for it in range(iters_limit):
       if time exceeded: break
       c = fft_convolve(cur_a)
       M = c.max()
       if not finite: break
       p = np.exp(-lam_cur * (1.0 - c/M))
       psum = p.sum()
       if psum <=0 or not finite: break
       p /= psum
       q = cross_corr(cur_a, p)
       q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
       meanq = q.mean()
       # If q uniform, no change
       ratio = meanq / q
       # cap ratio to avoid huge
       max_ratio = 1e6
       ratio = np.clip(ratio, 1e-6, max_ratio)
       cand = cur_a * np.power(ratio, alpha_cur)
       cand = normalize(cand, min_floor=1e-300)
       sc = score_fast(cand)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           # accept; maybe if much worse than current? only improve global
           cur_a = cand
           best_a = cand.copy()
           best_sc = sc
           bad=0
           # maybe increase alpha slightly? keep
       else:
           # no improvement; try smaller alpha
           cur_a = cur_a # or cand maybe if sc <= current_sc? We can update current if sc <= score(cur_a) plus small to have progress
           # To avoid excessive, if sc <= score_cur + 1e-6, accept as current but not best?
           score_cur = score_fast(cur_a) maybe expensive; we can compute current sc each loop? We can maintain.
```
We need manage local current score. Let's maintain `cur_sc = score_fast(cur_a)`. At each step compute cand_sc. If cand_sc < cur_sc: accept as current (even if not global best? Random perturbation function:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.2):
    n=len(a)
    x=np.log(a + 1e-300)
    # choose mode
    r=rng.random()
    if r<0.5:
       width=int(rng.integers(n//20, n//4+1)); start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width)); x[start:start+width]+=rng.normal(0, strength)
    elif r<0.7:
       x += rng.normal(0, strength*0.3, size=n)
    else:
       # tail linear ramp
       ramp=np.linspace(0,1,n); x += rng.normal(0,strength)* (np.where(r>0.99,1,-1))*ramp
    new=np.exp(x - x.max()); new=normalize(new)
    return new
```

Let's implement q cross-correlation in an efficient top-level function. Use `np.fft.rfft` and `irfft`. Ensure L power 2:
```
def _next_power_two(m):
    p=1
    while p<m: p <<=1
    return p
```
For n=2535, 2n-1=5069, L=8192. Good.

Function:
```
def corr_active(a, w):
    n=a.size; L=_next_power_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    wr=w[::-1]
    fw=np.fft.rfft(wr, L)
    full=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw, L)[:2*n-1]
    return 2.0 * full[::-1][:n]
```
Let's verify factor 2 not needed for ratio; constant cancels. But include.

We need ensure `w` length 2n-1. `w[::-1]` okay.

`fft_convolve`:
```
def fft_convolve(a):
    n=a.size; L=_next_power_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    return c
```
For score, if `c` has small imaginary no.

Could use `scipy.fft.rfft` for next_fast_len; but numpy supports n only power of 2. Power of 2 fast enough.

Algorithm details for `flatten_loop`:
```
def flatten_loop(start_a, lam_start, alpha_start, time_limit, rng, best_a_out, best_sc_out):
    a = start_a.copy()
    cur_sc = score_fast(a)
    best_a = start_a.copy(); best_sc = cur_sc
    bad = 0
    alpha = alpha_start
    lam = lam_start
    global best? We pass by returning.
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < time_limit:
        iter +=1
        c = fft_convolve(a)
        M = float(c.max())
        if M <=0 or not finite: break
        gap = 1.0 - c / M
        # avoid under/overflow: gap nonneg, clamp
        gap = np.clip(gap,0,None)
        p = np.exp(-lam * gap)
        psum = p.sum()
        if psum < 1e-300 or not finite: break
        p /= psum
        q = corr_active(a, p)
        q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
        meanq = float(q.mean())
        if not finite(meanq): break
        ratio = meanq / q
        np.clip(ratio, 1e-6, 1e6, out=ratio)
        cand = a * np.power(ratio, alpha)
        cand = normalize(cand)
        cand_sc = score_fast(cand)
        if cand_sc < cur_sc - 1e-12:
            a = cand; cur_sc = cand_sc
            if cand_sc < best_sc:
                best_a = cand.copy(); best_sc = cand_sc
            bad = 0
            # maybe nudge alpha up if improving consistently? Could set alpha = min(1.0, alpha*1.05) to converge faster.
        else:
            # if cand_sc only slightly worse, we may still move to escape? Use simulated annealing maybe.
            bad += 1
            alpha *= 0.7
            if alpha < 0.05:
                alpha = 0.5
                lam = cyclev
            if bad > 25:
                # perturb
                a = perturb(a, rng, 0.1)
                cur_sc = score_fast(a)
                bad=0
                # maybe if no global, reset? Keep current
    return best_a,best_sc
```
We need avoid infinite if lambda fixed. We can cycle lam if bad high: maybe in outer search call with different lam; inside, if bad high and no improvement after many, reset from best and try different lam? To keep simple, outer loops call flatten_loop with different lam/alpha. Flatten_loop itself can have one perturbation for escape.

Let's implement helper `conv_and_fa(a)`, and `corr_from_fa(p, fa, L)`:
```
def conv_and_fa(a):
    n=a.size; L=_next_power_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(c,0,out=c)
    return c, fa, L

def corr_from_fa(p, fa, L):
    n = (L//2)+1? Need n passed. We'll pass n.
    pr=p[::-1]
    fp=np.fft.rfft(pr,L)
    full=np.fft.irfft(fa*fp,L)[:2*n-1]
    return 2.0*full[::-1][:n]
```
For cand score, call `cand_c, _, _ = conv_and_fa(cand); sc=2*n*cand_c.max()`.

- Keep current c,fa,L,cur_sc.
- At iteration start, if c None compute.
- Use current c for p.
- Compute cand from q.
- Compute cand_c, cand_fa, L2 (same L), cand_sc.
- If accept: a=cand; c=cand_c; fa=cand_fa; cur_sc=cand_sc.
This requires storing fa. Good.

Let's write `flatten_loop` with c,fa reuse.

```
def flatten_loop(start_a, lam, alpha, time_limit, rng):
    a=orient(normalize(start_a))
    n=a.size
    c, fa, L = conv_and_fa(a)
    cur_sc=2*n*c.max()
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur_sc
    bad=0
    while time < time_limit:
       M=c.max()
       if M<=0: break
       gap=1-c/M; p=np.exp(-lam*gap); p /= p.sum()
       q=corr_from_fa(p, fa, L, n)
       ratio=np.clip(1/q.mean()/q? wait meanq/q)
       cand=a*np.power(ratio, alpha)
       cand=normalize(cand)
       cand_c,cand_fa,_=conv_and_fa(cand)
       cand_sc=2*n*cand_c.max()
       if cand_sc < cur_sc - 1e-12:
          a,c,fa,cur_sc = cand,cand_c,cand_fa,cand_sc
          if cand_sc < best_sc: best=...; best_sc=...
          bad=0
       else:
          bad +=1
          alpha *= 0.8
          if bad>30: perturb; recompute c? Maybe set a=perturb, compute conv, cur_sc, bad=0.
          # cycle lambda? maybe
    return best,best_sc
```

```
def normalize(a, target=1.0, floor=1e-300):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a, floor)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s<=0 or not finite: return np.ones(a.size,dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    return a*(target/s)
```
But if we want allow zeros, floor positive may bias. 1e-300 effectively zero but positive; if sum of floors n*1e-300 negligible. Good.

```
def lp_direction(f):
    n=f.size
    m=2*n-1
    try:
       A=np.empty((m,n), dtype=np.float64)
       idx=np.arange(n)
       for d in range(n):

       res = opt.linprog(-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.ones(m), bounds=(0, upper), method='highs', options={'time_limit': 30, 'presolve': True})

    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```

```
def lp_iteration(f_norm, time_limit):
    n=f_norm.size
    c=fft_convolve(f_norm); M=c.max()
    if M<=0: return f_norm
    f_scaled = f_norm / np.sqrt(M)  # peak1, sum maybe
    # orient? f_scaled first >= last? If reverse, constraints change but score same; do orient before.
    f_scaled=orient(f_scaled)
    g=solve_lp(f_scaled, time_limit/2)
    if g is None: return f_norm
    gs=g.sum()
    if gs<=0: return f_norm
    g_norm=normalize(g)
    # line search
    best=f_norm; best_sc=score_fast(f_norm)
    for t in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.75,1.0]:
       cand=(1-t)*f_norm + t*g_norm
       cand=normalize(np.maximum(cand,0))
       sc=score_fast(cand)
       if sc<best_sc: best=cand; best_sc=sc
    return best
```

In `_flatten_loop`:
```
def _flatten_loop(start_a, lam, alpha, deadline, rng):
    a = _orient(_normalize(start_a))
    n = a.size
    c, fa, L = _conv_and_fa(a)
    cur_sc = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c)) if c.size else inf
    best_a = a.copy(); best_sc = cur_sc
    bad=0
    alpha_cur=alpha
    lam_cur=lam
    # local exact not called
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       M=...
       if not finite: break
       gap = 1.0 - c/M
       gap=np.maximum(gap,0)
       # To avoid all zero, add small base
       p = np.exp(-lam_cur * gap)
       p += 1e-6
       psum=p.sum()
       if not finite(psum) or psum<=0: break
       p /= psum
       q = _corr_from_fa(p, fa, L, n)
       q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
       meanq = float(np.mean(q))
       if not finite(meanq) or meanq<=0: break
       ratio = meanq / q
       np.clip(ratio, 1e-6, 1e6, out=ratio)
       cand = a * np.power(ratio, alpha_cur)
       cand = _normalize(cand)
       cand_c, cand_fa, _ = _conv_and_fa(cand)
       cand_sc = 2.0*n*float(np.max(cand_c))
       if np.isfinite(cand_sc) and cand_sc < cur_sc - 1e-12:
           a = cand; c = cand_c; fa = cand_fa; cur_sc = cand_sc
           if cand_sc < best_sc:
               best_a = a.copy(); best_sc = cand_sc
           bad = 0
           # if improving a lot, maybe not change alpha; if many improve, can increase alpha a bit
           # but keep simple
       else:
           bad += 1
           alpha_cur *= 0.75
           if alpha_cur < 0.05:
               alpha_cur = 0.7
           if bad > 40:
               # perturb current or restart from best
               if rng.random() < 0.5:
                   a = _random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.1)
               else:
                   a = _random_perturb(a, rng, 0.2)
               c, fa, L = _conv_and_fa(a)
               cur_sc = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c))
               if not finite: break
               bad=0
               # maybe change lambda
               lam_cur = max(5.0, min(200.0, lam_cur * (1.2 if lam_cur<50 else 0.6)))
    return best_a, best_sc
```
If `cand_sc` is NaN due to FFT errors, reject.

`_random_perturb`: Need no lambda.
```
def _random_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.2):
    n=a.size
    x=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300))
    mode = rng.random()
    if mode <0.4:
       width = int(rng.integers(max(1,n//50), max(2,n//8)))
       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width)))
       x[start:start+width] += rng.normal(0.0, strength)
    elif mode<0.7:
       x += rng.normal(0.0, strength*0.3, n)
    else:
       # ramp tail or head
       inc = 1.0 if rng.random()<0.7 else -1.0
       ramp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
       x += inc * strength * ramp
    new = np.exp(x - np.max(x))
    return _normalize(new)
```
Need ensure `width` valid: `max(1,n//50)` could exceed `n//8` if n small; but n large. Use:
```
lo = max(1, n//50)
hi = max(lo+1, n//4)
width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi))
start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width)))
```
If n-width<=0, set 0.

```
def _shape_search(base, n, deadline, rng):
    start_best = base.copy(); best_sc = _conv_score(start_best)
    x = np.linspace(0,1,n) if n>1 else np.array([0.0])
    # A,B lists
    A_vals = [-2.0,-1.0,-0.5,0.0,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0]
    B_vals = [0.0,0.5,1.0,2.0]
    starts=[]
    # evaluate all; store if finite
    for A in A_vals:
       for B in B_vals:
          if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
          a = base * np.exp(A*x + B*x*x)
          a = _normalize(a)
          sc = _conv_score(a)
          if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
              best_sc=sc; start_best=a.copy()
          # store top? not here
    return start_best, best_sc
```

```
def _conv_score(a):
    n=a.size
```

Detailed code:
```
"""Search for nonnegative coefficients minimizing ..."""
import time, math
import numpy as np
try:
    import scipy.optimize as _scipy_opt
except Exception:
    _scipy_opt = None
```

Helper `_half_binomial`: recurrence. Ensure values finite. If n<=0 return ones.
```
def _half_binomial(n):
    a=np.empty(n,float)
    if n<=0: return a
    v=1.0
    a[0]=v
    for i in range(n-1):
       v *= (2.0*i + 1.0)/(2.0*i + 2.0)
       a[i+1]=v
    return a
```
For i=0 ->0.5.

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0) # nan_to_num available
    a=np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
       return np.full(a.size, target/max(1,a.size))
    return a*(target/s)
```
`math.isfinite` on float. If a.size 0, return empty? We won't use. If target maybe 1.

`_orient`:
```
def _orient(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size > 1 and a[-1] > a[0]:
        a = a[::-1]
    return a.copy()
```
Return copy maybe.

`_next_power_two`:
```
def _next_power_two(m):
    p=1
    while p < m:
       p <<=1
    return p
```

`_conv_and_fa`:
```
def _conv_and_fa(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([]), np.array([],complex), 1
    L=_next_power_two(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    return c, fa, L
```
Complex array `fa` maybe; returning np.empty complex? okay.

`_conv_score` general:
```
def _conv_score(a):
    n=a.size
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if n==0 or s<=0 or not math.isfinite(s): return float('inf')
    c,_,_= _conv_and_fa(a)
    if c.size==0: return inf
    mx=float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(mx): return inf
    return 2.0*n*mx/(s*s)
```

`_corr_from_fa`:
```
def _corr_from_fa(p, fa, L, n):
    pw = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], L)
    full = np.fft.irfft(fa * pw, L)[:2*n-1]
    return 2.0 * full[::-1][:n]
```
`fa` is rfft of a. Good.

`_random_perturb`: as above. Need `rng.integers` with half-open; ensure hi>lo.
```
lo=max(1,n//50); hi=max(lo+1,int(n*0.25)); width=int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))? integers hi exclusive; use hi=lo+1 max... Let's do hi=min(n, lo+max(1,n//4)); width=rng.integers(lo, hi+1). start=rng.integers(0, max(1,n-width+1))? For end index start+width<=n, so start max n-width. `int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))` if n-width=0, max1. Use if n-width>0 else 0.
```
Better:
```
hi = min(n, lo + max(1, n//4))
width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi+1))
if n-width <=0: start=0
else: start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
```
`rng.integers(0, n-width+1)` if n-width+1>0.
For random ramp, `np.linspace` okay.

`_flatten_loop`: Use math.isfinite. In loop computing p, `gap = 1.0 - c / M`; due to roundoff gap negative tiny; clip. `p = np.exp(-lam_cur * gap) + 1e-6`. If lam_cur high and gap maybe >700? gap <=1, okay.
`ratio = meanq / q`; if q very small ratio huge; clip.
`np.power(ratio, alpha_cur, out=?)` Could do `cand = a * np.power(ratio, alpha_cur)`. If alpha 1, okay. If alpha 0.8. For large arrays, okay.
Accept/simulated annealing:
```
if np.isfinite(cand_sc) and cand_sc < cur_sc - 1e-12:
   accept
else:
   bad +=1
   alpha_cur *=0.78
   if alpha_cur < 0.05: alpha_cur = 0.6
   if bad > 30:
       if rng.random() < 0.35 and cand_sc < cur_sc * 1.05:
           # accept bad mildly
       elif rng.random() < 0.7:
           a = _random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.15)
       else:
           a = _random_perturb(a, rng, 0.3)
```

```
def _exact_update(a, best_score, best_seq):
    if a is None or a.size==0: return best_score,best_seq
    arr=_normalize(a) # ensure sum1; but if best_seq should be exact, don't change? Use original? Normalized sum1 okay.
    # For evaluator, values maybe floor positive. Convert.
    # If sum <0.01? sum1.
    try:
       val=evaluate_sequence([float(max(0.0, float(v))) for v in arr])
    except Exception:
       return best_score,best_seq
    try: val=float(val)
    except: return ...
    if math.isfinite(val) and val < best_score:
       return val, [float(max(0.0, float(v))) for v in arr]
    return best_score,best_seq
```

`_solve_lp`:
```
def _solve_lp(f, time_limit):
    if _scipy_opt is None: return None
    n=f.size
    if n <=0 or n > 2200: return None # memory cap
    m=2*n-1
    try:
       A=np.zeros((m,n), dtype=np.float64)
       idx=np.arange(n)
       for d in range(n):
           A[d+idx, idx] = float(f[d])
       upper = 1.0 / max(float(np.max(f)), 1e-300)
       if not math.isfinite(upper) or upper <=0: upper=1e6
       c=-np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)
       b=np.ones(m,dtype=np.float64)
       options={'time_limit': max(1,int(time_limit)), 'output_flag': 0, 'presolve': True}
       res=_scipy_opt.linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=(0, upper), method='highs', options=options)
       if res is not None and res.status in (0,):
           g=np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
           g=np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
           g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
           if g.sum() > 0:
               return g
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
`output_flag` maybe accepted; if not, exception. To be safe, use `options={'time_limit': int(...), 'presolve': True}`. `output_flag` may be valid. If invalid, linprog error. Use minimal.

If `time_limit` less than maybe 30; min 1.

`_lp_iteration`:
```
def _lp_iteration(f_input, time_limit):
    f=_orient(_normalize(f_input))
    n=f.size
    if n==0 or time_limit <=0: return f
    c,_,_= _conv_and_fa(f)
    M=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else inf
    if not math.isfinite(M) or M <=0: return f
    f_scaled = f / math.sqrt(M)
    f_scaled = _orient(f_scaled)
    g=_solve_lp(f_scaled, max(5, time_limit-20))
    if g is None: return f
    g_norm=_normalize(g)
    best=f; best_sc=_conv_score(f)
    ts=[0.01,0.02,0.03,0.05,0.08,0.1,0.15,0.2,0.3,0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0]
    for t in ts:
       cand = (1.0-t)*f + t*g_norm
       cand = _normalize(np.maximum(cand,0.0))
       sc = _conv_score(cand)
       if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
           best=cand; best_sc=sc
    return best
```
If f/n orientation changed; f normalized sum1. Good.

Final code: (will be lengthy)

```python
"""
Search for non-negative coefficients minimizing
    score(a) = 2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

The program uses:
  - a strong analytic starting point: the truncated series of (1-z)^(-1/2),
    whose square is exactly flat for its first n lags;
  - a low-dimensional shape scan around that starting point;
  - a mirror-descent / multiplicative flattening iteration whose weights are
    adapted from the current convolution peak(s);
  - an optional linear-programming direction based on the previous state of the
    art (maximize sum(g) subject to f*g <= M);
  - periodic exact validation with the authoritative evaluate_sequence().

All helpers are top-level and use no closures or lambdas.
"""

import time
import math
import numpy as np

try:
    import scipy.optimize as _scipy_opt
except Exception:
    _scipy_opt = None


def _half_binomial(n):
    """Coefficients of (1-z)^(-1/2), i.e. C(2i,i)/4^i."""
    a = np.empty(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    v = 1.0
    a[0] = v
    for i in range(a.size - 1):
        v *= (2.0 * i + 1.0) / (2.0 * i + 2.0)
        a[i + 1] = v
    return a


def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    """Clamp to non-negative finite values and scale to target sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(a.size, target / max(1, a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (target / s)


def _orient(a):
    """Reverse if the last entry is larger; score is invariant under reversal."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size > 1 and a[-1] > a[0]:
        a = a[::-1]
    return np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()


def _next_power_two(m):
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _conv_and_fa(a):
    """FFT convolution a*a (full) and the rFFT of a for reuse."""
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64), np.array([], dtype=np.complex128), 1
    L = _next_power_two(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)[:2 * n - 1]
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c, fa, L


def _conv_score(a):
    """Exact (FFT-based) score for an arbitrary non-negative vector."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    c, _, _ = _conv_and_fa(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not math.isfinite(mx):
        return float("inf")
    return (2.0 * float(n) * mx) / (s * s)


def _corr_from_fa(p, fa, L, n):
    """
    For weights p[k] on convolution lags, return
        2 * sum_j a[j] * p[i+j]
    for i=0,...,n-1, using the already computed rfft(a)=fa.
    """
    pw = np.fft.rfft(p[::-1], L)
    full = np.fft.irfft(fa * pw, L)[:2 * n - 1]
    return 2.0 * full[::-1][:n]


def _random_perturb(a, rng, strength=0.2):
    """Random positive multiplicative perturbation in log-space."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    mode = float(rng.random())
    if mode < 0.45:
        lo = max(1, n // 50)
        hi = min(n, lo + max(1, n // 4))
        width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
        if n - width <= 0:
            start = 0
        else:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        x[start:start + width] += rng.normal(0.0, strength)
    elif mode < 0.75:
        x += rng.normal(0.0, 0.3 * strength, n)
    else:
        inc = 1.0 if rng.random() < 0.7 else -1.0
        ramp = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        x += inc * strength * ramp
    x -= float(np.max(x))
    return _normalize(np.exp(x))


def _flatten_loop(start_a, lam, alpha, deadline, rng):
    """
    Local mirror-descent flattening search.

    It adaptively weights near-maximal convolution coefficients and pushes mass
    out of coordinates with high weighted derivative 2*(p circ a).
    """
    a = _orient(_normalize(np.asarray(start_a, dtype=np.float64)))
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    c, fa, L = _conv_and_fa(a)
    cur_sc = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_sc = cur_sc

    alpha_cur = float(alpha)
    lam_cur = float(lam)
    bad = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if c.size == 0:
            break
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if not math.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break

        gap = 1.0 - c / M
        gap = np.maximum(gap, 0.0)
        p = np.exp(-lam_cur * gap)
        p += 1e-6  # avoid perfectly one-hot weights
        psum = float(p.sum())
        if not math.isfinite(psum) or psum <= 0.0:
            break
        p /= psum

        q = _corr_from_fa(p, fa, L, n)
        q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
        meanq = float(np.mean(q))
        if not math.isfinite(meanq) or meanq <= 0.0:
            break

        ratio = meanq / q
        np.clip(ratio, 1e-6, 1e6, out=ratio)

        # Trial a small set of step sizes to make hill-climbing more stable.
        trial_alphas = [alpha_cur]
        if alpha_cur > 0.10:
            trial_alphas.append(alpha_cur * 0.5)
        if alpha_cur < 0.85:
            trial_alphas.append(min(1.0, alpha_cur * 1.8))

        best_cand = None
        best_cand_c = None
        best_cand_fa = None
        best_cand_sc = cur_sc
        used_alpha = alpha_cur

        for al in trial_alphas:
            al = float(min(1.0, max(0.01, al)))
            cand = a * np.power(ratio, al)
            cand = _normalize(cand)
            cand_c, cand_fa, _ = _conv_and_fa(cand)
            cand_sc = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(cand_c))
            if math.isfinite(cand_sc) and cand_sc < best_cand_sc:
                best_cand = cand
                best_cand_c = cand_c
                best_cand_fa = cand_fa
                best_cand_sc = cand_sc
                used_alpha = al

        if best_cand is not None and best_cand_sc < cur_sc - 1e-12:
            a = best_cand
            c = best_cand_c
            fa = best_cand_fa
            cur_sc = best_cand_sc
            alpha_cur = used_alpha
            bad = 0
            if cur_sc < best_sc:
                best_a = a.copy()
                best_sc = cur_sc
        else:
            bad += 1
            alpha_cur *= 0.78
            if alpha_cur < 0.04:
                alpha_cur = 0.45

            # Simulated-annealing-like mild acceptance to avoid tiny basins.
            if bad > 12 and rng.random() < 0.12:
                mild = best_cand if best_cand is not None else a
                mild_sc = best_cand_sc if best_cand_c is not None else cur_sc
                if (math.isfinite(mild_sc) and mild_sc < cur_sc * 1.05
                        and mild_sc <= best_sc * 1.20):
                    a = mild
                    c = best_cand_c if best_cand_c is not None else c
                    fa = best_cand_fa if best_cand_fa is not None else fa
                    cur_sc = mild_sc
                    alpha_cur = used_alpha
                    bad = 0
                    continue

            if bad > 35:
                if rng.random() < 0.65:
                    a = _random_perturb(best_a, rng, 0.12)
                else:
                    a = _random_perturb(a, rng, 0.25)
                c, fa, L = _conv_and_fa(a)
                cur_sc = 2.0 * float(n) * float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
                if not math.isfinite(cur_sc):
                    break
                bad = 0
                lam_cur = max(5.0, min(220.0, lam_cur * (1.35 if lam_cur < 60.0 else 0.55)))

    return best_a, best_sc


def _shape_search(base, n, deadline, rng):
    """Quick low-dimensional scan around the half-binomial shape."""
    a0 = _orient(_normalize(base))
    best = a0.copy()
    best_sc = _conv_score(a0)

    if n > 1:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    else:
        x = np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)

    A_list = [-2.0, -1.0, -0.5, -0.25, 0.0, 0.1, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 4.0, 7.0]
    B_list = [0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0]

    for A in A_list:
        for B in B_list:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                return best, best_sc
            fac = np.exp(A * x + B * x * x)
            cand = _orient(_normalize(base * fac))
            sc = _conv_score(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best = cand
                best_sc = sc

    for _ in range(35):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        A = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.4))
        B = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.55))
        fac = np.exp(A * x + B * x * x)
        cand = _orient(_normalize(base * fac))
        sc = _conv_score(cand)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best = cand
            best_sc = sc

    return best, best_sc


def _exact_update(a, best_score, best_seq):
    """Validate with the authoritative evaluator and update best if improved."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return best_score, best_seq
    a = _normalize(a)
    seq = [float(max(0.0, float(v))) for v in a]
    try:
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
    except Exception:
        return best_score, best_seq
    try:
        val = float(val)
    except Exception:
        return best_score, best_seq
    if math.isfinite(val) and val < best_score:
        return val, seq
    return best_score, best_seq


def _solve_lp(f, time_limit):
    """
    LP direction:
        maximize sum(g)
        subject to f*g <= 1, g >= 0.
    Here f is scaled so that ||f*f||_inf = 1.
    """
    if _scipy_opt is None:
        return None
    n = int(f.size)
    if n <= 0 or n > 2200:
        return None
    m = 2 * n - 1
    try:
        A = np.zeros((m, n), dtype=np.float64)
        idx = np.arange(n)
        for d in range(n):
            A[d + idx, idx] = float(f[d])

        fmax = float(np.max(f))
        if not math.isfinite(fmax) or fmax <= 0.0:
            return None
        upper = 1.0 / fmax
        if not math.isfinite(upper) or upper <= 0.0:
            upper = 1e6

        c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
        options = {
            "time_limit": max(1, int(time_limit)),
            "presolve": True,
        }
        res = _scipy_opt.linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            bounds=(0.0, float(upper)),
            method="highs",
            options=options,
        )
        if res is not None and int(res.status) == 0:
            g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
            g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
            if float(g.sum()) > 0.0:
                return g
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def _lp_iteration(f_input, time_limit):
    """One LP-guided blend step, followed by a line search."""
    f = _orient(_normalize(np.asarray(f_input, dtype=np.float64)))
    n = int(f.size)
    if n == 0 or time_limit <= 0.0:
        return f

    c, _, _ = _conv_and_fa(f)
    M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else float("inf")
    if not math.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
        return f

    f_scaled = f / math.sqrt(M)
    f_scaled = _orient(f_scaled)

    g = _solve_lp(f_scaled, max(5.0, time_limit - 15.0))
    if g is None:
        return f

    g_norm = _normalize(g)
    best = f
    best_sc = _conv_score(f)

    t_values = [0.01, 0.02, 0.03, 0.05, 0.08, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20,
                0.30, 0.40, 0.60, 0.80, 1.00]
    for t in t_values:
        cand = (1.0 - float(t)) * f + float(t) * g_norm
        cand = _normalize(np.maximum(cand, 0.0))
        sc = _conv_score(cand)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best = cand
            best_sc = sc
    return best


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Return a list of non-negative coefficients approximating the lower bound."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    if not math.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0.0:
        budget = 1800.0
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, budget - 8.0)

    parent = None
    try:
        parent = height_sequence_1  # noqa: F821
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    # Use a technically comfortable length.  2048 is large enough for the
    # target region, but still allows fast FFTs and one exact LP if desired.
    n = 2048
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            plen = int(len(parent))
            if plen > 0:
                if plen < 1024:
                    n = 1024
                elif plen > 2048:
                    n = 2048
                else:
                    n = plen
        except Exception:
            pass

    base = _half_binomial(n)
    x_lin = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n) if n > 1 else np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)

    # 1) Initial shape scan.
    initial_deadline = min(deadline, t0 + max(5.0, min(60.0, 0.035 * budget)))
    best_a, best_sc = _shape_search(base, n, initial_deadline, rng)

    # If the provided parent has the same length, let it compete once.
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            plen = int(len(parent))
            if plen == n:
                pa = _orient(_normalize(np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)))
                pa_sc = _conv_score(pa)
                if math.isfinite(pa_sc) and pa_sc < best_sc:
                    best_a = pa
                    best_sc = pa_sc
        except Exception:
            pass

    print("initial internal score:", best_sc, "n:", n, flush=True)

    # 2) Exact validation of the current best.
    best_exact_score = float("inf")
    best_exact_seq = None
    best_exact_score, best_exact_seq = _exact_update(best_a, best_exact_score, best_exact_seq)
    print("initial exact score:", best_exact_score, flush=True)

    # 3) Optional LP-guided step.
    if n <= 2048:
        lp_avail = deadline - 90.0 - time.monotonic()
        if lp_avail > 60.0:
            lp_deadline = min(deadline - 90.0, time.monotonic() + min(180.0, lp_avail))
            try:
                a_lp = _lp_iteration(best_a, max(1.0, lp_deadline - time.monotonic()))
                sc_lp = _conv_score(a_lp)
                if math.isfinite(sc_lp) and sc_lp < best_sc:
                    best_a = a_lp
                    best_sc = sc_lp
                    print("LP improved internal score:", best_sc, flush=True)
                    best_exact_score, best_exact_seq = _exact_update(
                        best_a, best_exact_score, best_exact_seq
                    )
                    print("LP exact score:", best_exact_score, flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # 4) Adaptive flattening local search with periodic exact checks.
    lam_list = [15.0, 30.0, 60.0, 100.0, 25.0, 45.0]
    alpha_list = [0.30, 0.60, 1.00, 0.75]
    chunk = 60.0
    idx = 0
    last_exact = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0:
        remain = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remain < 12.0:
            break
        slice_time = min(chunk, remain)
        idx += 1

        lam = float(lam_list[idx % len(lam_list)])
        alpha = float(alpha_list[idx % len(alpha_list)])

        # Occasionally jump to a randomly tilted shape to avoid a single basin.
        if idx % 6 == 0:
            A = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.5))
            B = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.6))
            start_a = _orient(_normalize(base * np.exp(A * x_lin + B * x_lin * x_lin)))
        else:
            strength = 0.04 + 0.18 * float(idx % 7) / 7.0
            start_a = _random_perturb(best_a, rng, strength)

        slice_deadline = min(deadline - 1.0, time.monotonic() + slice_time)
        cur_best, cur_sc = _flatten_loop(start_a, lam, alpha, slice_deadline, rng)

        improved = False
        if math.isfinite(cur_sc) and cur_sc < best_sc:
            best_a = cur_best
            best_sc = cur_sc
            improved = True
            print(
                "flattening improvement, lam:", lam,
                "alpha:", alpha,
                "internal:", best_sc,
                flush=True
            )
            best_exact_score, best_exact_seq = _exact_update(
                best_a, best_exact_score, best_exact_seq
            )
            print("current exact score:", best_exact_score, flush=True)
            last_exact = time.monotonic()

        # Even without an internal improvement, exact-check moderately often.
        if not improved and time.monotonic() - last_exact > 20.0:
            best_exact_score, best_exact_seq = _exact_update(
                best_a, best_exact_score, best_exact_seq
            )
            last_exact = time.monotonic()

    # Final exact validation.
    best_exact_score, best_exact_seq = _exact_update(
        best_a, best_exact_score, best_exact_seq
    )
    print("final internal score:", best_sc, flush=True)
    print("final exact score:", best_exact_score, flush=True)

    if best_exact_seq is not None and math.isfinite(best_exact_score):
        return best_exact_seq

    arr = _normalize(best_a)
    return [float(max(0.0, float(v))) for v in arr]
```
